GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS. 

1. On two or more tracks, extra trains may run, work extras m work or run with the current of traffic on the authority of Trai masters, Assistant Trainmasters, Yard Masters, or Operators, and m occupy main tracks under proper protection, until the arrival of four class trains and other extras. Fourth class trains and other extras w be governed accordingly. 

2. Trains using middle sidings must, unless otherwise directed, ent siding at first switch, and, where middle crossovers are provided, lea siding at crossover. 

3. (a) Class K-1, T-1, I-10, E-5 and N-1 engines must not be r over coal trestles. GEN The (b) All Road Enginemen and Conductors will be required to hay with them, while on duty, copies of all current General Orders for period of not less than two weeks after the effective date of the orde covering any division or portion of a division over which they operat 

Book Form 364-1 or 2, in which all qualified Road Enginemen an

Conductors, whether or not serving in that capacity, are required sign for General Orders, will be supplemented with printed or stampe notations: "I have read and understand, and herewith sign for Ger eral Order ( )". 

Yard Conductors and Enginemen are to be familiar with and unde stand General Orders that affect the territory over which they operat 

Road Conductors and Enginemen must sign, before commencin any trip or tour of duty, and insert in the space provided on the time return Form 475 and 477, the number of the latest General Order fo each division over which they are to operate. This signature sha be certifications that they have not only read and signed for all Ger eral Orders as shown, but that they have in their possession an understand the provisions of the current General Orders. 

Before starting on a trip or tour of duty, the Conductor and E gineman must know that the other is qualified and has current Gener Orders for such trip or tour of duty. In addition, the Conducto must know, with respect to the Trainmen, and the Engineer, wit respect to the Fireman, that they have seen and are familiar wit 

General Orders. 

4. In Automatic Block Signal territory, permission must be obtaine before trains or engines enter main track or cross over from one mai track to another. Such permission will not relieve crews from com plying with the requirements of Rule 86.

Trains or engines passing from side tracks to main tracks or crossin over from one main track to another must, as a precaution agains following trains, be governed by Operating Rule 513, except at- (a) Meeting points. (b) American Street Line from Tabor Junction to Fairhill Junction (c) Tamaqua Yard (within yard limits).

ENT (d) Pottsville Yard from Pottsville Junction to passenger station. (e) Bethlehem Yard from Adams Street to West end of Bethle hem Passenger Station. (f) Newberry Junction Yard from Signal No. 150 located four hundred fifty-seven (457) feet east of Arch Street, Newberry to end of automatic block located one thousand four hun dred ninety-one (1491) feet west of Arch Street, Newberry 5. When coupling occupied passenger equipment a stop must b made about ten (10) feet distant and then move slowly to make the coupling.

The steam, air or whistle hose must never be connected until the cars have been stretched to assure that the coupling has been made. 

Cars will be coupled and air brakes working when doing work with occupied passenger equipment. 

Trains making back-up moves with passenger equipment, must have back-up hose or platform valve in operation and exercise care when approaching public crossings and passing through yards. The air signal whistle to be sounded at intervals at such points. 

Passenger equipment must not be detached while cars are in motion and when switching, must be shoved into track and stopped before detached.

If and when passenger carrying equipment is handled in mixed cal freight trains, such passenger cars will be carried on rear of S. the case of thru or local high speed passenger trains carrying ht equipment in Pennsylvania, the latter will be carried on rear of s except in cases where freight cars are especially equipped for enger service, (having proper trucks, wheels, air, signal and steam ections, etc.) they may be carried on head end.

ELECTRICAL OPERATION. 

Conditions Affecting the Power System to be Reported to the 

Power Dispatcher. 

he Power Dispatcher is located at Wayne Junction Substation has control of all electrical lines and substations within electrified itory. He receives and dispatches all trouble reports in connection rewith. 

When emergency requires that power be shut off the overhead enary system, immediately telephone the Power Dispatcher or in Dispatcher. Power will not be restored until the Power Discher has been notified by a responsible person that it is safe to do so.

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

TER Do not touch any overhead wires even though the Power Dispat reports that he has cut off the power, unless a qualified electr employe is present and has placed a visible ground connection that wire. Any wire of the overhead system without a visible grom connection must be considered alive at all times and coming in cont with such a wire may cause serious injury. 

Employes must not touch dangling wires nor attempt to m them by any means, but must report their location immediately the Power Dispatcher and should, if possible, leave someone to wa such wires until their removal. Other persons in danger should warned of their location. 

Employes must report to the Power Dispatcher any attachme of the overhead wire system which are out of place and state whet or not they are in such a position that they can foul the pantogra of an electric car or the top of a steam locomotive. 

Loose connections to traction and impedance bonds must be regard as alive and report must be made promptly to the Power Dispatch- Employes observing excessive flashes or arcs at overhead bridg flashes on or about the car or overhead equipment indicating so1irregularity in the electrical operation should immediately notify t 

Train Dispatcher or Power Dispatcher. Delay in reporting su cases may result in damage to equipment or train delays.

In reporting troubles to the overhead system it will assist mai tenance forces if proper terms for the various parts are used. The terms are shown in the view of line construction included in th time table. 

(2) Qualified Employes. 

Whenever the term Qualified Employes is used in the followin instructions it refers to those employes in the electric service who b examination and approval of their superior officers are qualified t perform certain duties and are classed as follows:

CLASS 1.-Employes competent to erect, maintain and repair electric apparatus or supervise and protect other employes pe forming such work.

CLASS 2.-Employes such as Enginemen operating electric equip ment, Electricians on Electric Rolling Equipment an other employes in electric service permitted to go o high equipment for the purposes and under condition hereinafter authorized. 

(3) Handling Work Near Overhead Wires. 

All overhead wires including catenary, transmission and signal line in electrified zones are to be considered alive at all times. Insulating covering of wire should not be depended upon for protection against shock. 

No employe except Class 1 employes shall do any work near high voltage wires or apparatus where it is possible for any part of his body or tools and material with which he is working to come within three feet of such wires, unless a Class 1 employe is assigned to protect him against personal injury. When persons other than Class 1 or

Class 2 employes are required to do work near overhead wires and apparatus they must be protected by a Class 1 employe who will take necessary precautions for their safety before starting and during progress of the work.

20 

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

e POF 

Keep Off Top of High Equipment. 

Employes (excepting Class 1 and Class 2 electric service employes others under their supervision in the discharge of their duties) prohibited from going on top of box cars, locomotives, tenders or er high equipment while on tracks electrified with high voltage erhead wires or while movements are being made to such tracks tem sidings, yards or other tracks which are not electrified. Com 

WHILE UNDER OR NEAR OVERHEAD BRIDGES ENGINE- EEN, FIREMEN AND OTHERS ARE PROHIBITED FROM DING ON FOOT BOARDS OR SMOKE BOX STEPS OF LOCO- OTIVES WHILE ON TRACKS ELECTRIFIED WITH HIGH OLTAGE OVERHEAD WIRES. At other points under the wires, emen and others are prohibited from going on foot boards or smoke x steps without first securing permission from the engineman, who Il supervise the movements of such employes while in such posi n. KEEP AT LEAST THREE (3) FEET FROM OVERHEAD IRES. 

When engine stops to take water, fireman must not climb over al in tender. He will alight from engine by way of side steps, walk the rear and use rear ladder to reach man-hole; this, to avoid ming in contact with overhead power wires.

Spout of water column must be operated by lever located on lumn. Fire hooks or other metal rods must not be used to turn ater spout.

Employes assigned to duty as pilots with foreign crews or crews other divisions using tracks electrified with high voltage overhead Tres must arrange to have each member of such crews notified that must not get on top of high equipment within the electrified zone. 

Yardmasters and Conductors will notify caretakers of equipment shipments, that they must not go on top of equipment within the ectrified zone. 

4) Locomotive Operation. 

It will be the specific duty of the engineman to know, before enterg any electrified zone, that no part of his engine or engine equiptent will foul the overhead catenary construction, particular attention eing given the position of cab ventilators. 

Firing tools must be handled in such manner that they can not me in contact with the overhead wires.

Care must be exercised when using the squirt hose on locomotives prevent the stream from striking the overhead wires. Squirt hose ust not be used when locomotives are in train sheds, or when assing under overhead bridges. 

To avoid failure of transmission and catenary insulators and the onsequent interruption to the power supply due to the action of team and smoke on these insulators, enginemen must, so far as ossible, avoid stopping their engines under porcelain or wood stick nsulators or under highway or other bridges within the electrified zone. 

6) Multiple Unit Operation.

Employes whose duty it is to operate multiple unit cars must amiliarize themselves with such equipment and carefully study the pecial instruction book on "Alternating Current Multiple Unit Car Equipment." 

The master controller handle must in no case be blocked, fastened or otherwise tampered with in such a manner as to prevent the spring tension returning it to the Emergency Position if the Engineman's hand is removed.

INSTRUCTIONS—(Con.). 

GENERAL 

When necessary for engineman to leave his compartment due trouble while on the road the brake valve and controller hand must be removed and kept in his possession. 

When two or more electric trains have been stopped on same tra a short distance apart, each engineman should not attempt to sta his train until 30 seconds after train ahead has started and th should accelerate slowly by notching up the controller.

In event of loss of power from the trolley wire, engineman mu immediately place controller handle in "off" position and keep there until power is restored. 

Signalmen, Yardmen or Trainmen must not line up tracks electric trains for movements to unwired tracks unless pantograp are down and all concerned are informed as to the movement to made.

Multiple unit trains must not assist in starting steam trains this would probably damage motors and electrical equipment. 

In cases where trolley wires are noticed to be shaking violent unusually heavy arcing occurs, or it is believed that electrical trou exists, all pantographs should be lowered at once and the Power D patcher advised of conditions immediately.

When there is a possibility that the contact between the multip unit car and the electric return circuit. as represented by the runni rail, may be broken due to derailment or any other cause, pantograp must be immediately lowered. No pantographs shall be raised un it has been definitely known that the multiple unit car is again maki proper contact with the rail return circuit.

In the event of a pantograph being fouled or damaged, the engin man assisted by the train crew must make prompt efforts to clear t trouble so that the train may proceed if possible. In the event 

Class 1 employe is not present, Class 2 employes and others under the supervision may clear the trouble by the following procedure:- (a) Pull pantograph down to the lock position either by means pantograph "Down" button or by use of hook stick. In the event th pantograph is damaged, pantograph or pantograph parts should removed from contact with the energized wires only by means of t hook stick. Sections must be removed to a point at least three fe below the level of the contact wire and clear any other energized portic of the overhead wire or fittings by this distance. (b) When possible, close grounding switch on damaged car aft bus connectors have been opened. (c) Class 2 employe must assure himself that in going to the c roof he will not place any part of his body, tools or equipment wit which he is working within three feet of the overhead wire or fitting 

UNDER NO CONSIDERATION, UNLESS THE FOREGOING 

INSTRUCTIONS (a), (b) and (c) ARE COMPLIED WITH, MA

CLASS 2 EMPLOYES OR OTHERS UNDER THEIR SUPER VISION GO ON THE CAR ROOF. In the event it is impossible t clear the pantograph from the wire or fittings by at least three feet b means of the hook stick, enginemen or train crew must communicat with Train Dispatcher requesting services of a Class 1 employe wh will ground overhead wires and clear pantograph to permit movemen of train.

INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

GENERAL 

n securing the damaged pantograph, the train must not be moved il the pantograph has been removed or properly secured so that no ts may come in contact with overhead wires or trains running on acent tracks. Bus connectors, on the particular car, should be sepaed, and pantograph damaged in any part should have grounding tch closed. 

When observing pantograph drop order, in effect over a certain tion of track, the pantograph down button should be pushed in and in until train has passed under the section in trouble. 

Pantographs on cars laying over must be kept down except when uired to be against wire to make necessary tests, heat cars or to pare them for movement.

Operation of Work Equipment and Maintenance of Way Ma chinery.

When derricks or cranes are used in electrified territory the wreck ster or work train foreman in charge of the equipment must take ecial care to safeguard the workmen and himself from the electrified erhead wires. The operation of the equipment must be conducted der the personal supervision of the wreckmaster or work train eman, who must be governed by the rules covering clearances as tlined below.

Track Supervisors and others assigning work trains containing derks, ditches, cranes, etc., for service at any location within limits of ectrified territory, shall notify Power Dispatcher of limits of work ea and working time of equipment prior to starting work. 

When Maintenance of Way Machinery equipped with boom is used electrified territory, the boom must be properly grounded. Such achinery must be operated so that the following clearance restricons are observed: 

With wires alive: 

1. Without supervision of Class 1 employes. 

-not closer than 5 feet to transmission wires. -not closer than 3 feet to catenary system or contact wire. -not closer than 3 feet to signal power wires. 

2. Under supervision of Class 1 employe. 

-not closer than 3 feet to transmission wires. -not closer than 14 inches to catenary system or contact wire. -not closer than 14 inches to signal power wires. ▪) With wires deenergized and grounded: 

1. Under supervision of Class 1 employe. 

-contact with wires permitted if necessary, avoiding damage. 

If in the opinion of the Foreman or Operator, above clearances canot be maintained or any hazards are involved, protection of a Class 1 mploye must be requested.

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS—(Con.). 

When equipment is required to work in vicinity of overhead bridg where wires are depressed below normal height of 22 feet, special p cautions must be taken by Foreman or Operator, and unless cle ances of Section a,1 can be met, no work shall proceed unless und protection of a Class 1 employe. (8) Fires within Electrified Territory.

When fires occur near overhead wires or when fire apparatus tested near live wires, the power should be cut off and the wi grounded.

Water must not be used to extinguish an electrical fire. Sar pyrene, and other extinguishers containing carbon tetrachloride m be used on electrical fires, on arcs, or other exposed live parts. 

Any employe noticing fires or other trouble on electric cars, wir poles or in manholes must immediately notify the Power Dispatch 

(9) Miscellaneous. 

All employes must familiarize themselves with rules for resuscitati from electric shock. 

"SAFETY FIRST" SHOULD BE EVER IN THE MIND O EACH EMPLOYE. 

STANDARD TECHNIQUE FOR EXECUTING THE BACK PRESSURE—ARM LIFT METHOD OF ARTIFICIAL RESPIRATION 

Follow these Instructions Even if the Patient Appears Dead.

The victim must be freed from contact with the live conductor promptly as possible. Use a dry stick, dry rope, dry coat, or oth non-conductor. Extreme care must be exercised in releasing t victim to avoid receiving a shock yourself. Many persons, by the carelessness in such matters, have been severely shocked or burne If alone with the victim, do not neglect immediate and continu resuscitation in order to call a doctor. START AT ONCE-TE

FIRST FEW MINUTES ARE VALUABLE. If other persons a present, send one of them for a doctor without a moment's dela The later treatment, given through medical assistance, requires fir of all a live patient; therefore, delay in employing artificial respirati may lose the fight at the very outset.

As soon as possible feel with your fingers in the patient's mouth a throat and remove any foreign body (tobacco, false teeth, etc.). the mouth is tight shut, pay no more attention to it until later. I not stop to loosen the patient's clothing, but immediately begin actu resuscitation. Every moment of delay is serious. Proceed as follo with:

Position of the Subject. 

(1) Place the subject in the face down, prone position. Bend h elbows and place the hands one upon the other. Turn his face to o side, placing the cheek upon his hands.

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

мул 

Osition of the Operator. 

(2) Kneel on either the right or left knee at the head of the subet facing him. Place the knee at the side of the subject's head close the forearm. Place the opposite foot near the elbow. If it is more mfortable, kneel on both knees, one on either side of the subject's ad. Place your hands upon the flat of the subject's back in such way that the heels lie just below a line running between the armpits. ith the tips of the thumbs just touching, spread the fingers downard and outward. ompression Phase (3) Rock forward until the arms are approximately vertical and low the weight of the upper part of your body to exert slow, steady, en pressure downward upon the hands. This forces air out of the ngs. Your elbows should be kept straight and the pressure exerted most directly downward on the back. "osition for Expansion Phase. (4) Release the pressure, avoiding a final thrust, and commence to 

Dck slowly backward. Place your hands upon the subject's arms st above his elbows.

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

Expansion Phase. 

(5) Draw his arms upward and toward you. Apply just enoug lift to feel resistance and tension at the subject's shoulders. Do n bend your elbows, and as you rock backward the subject's arms wi be drawn toward you. Then drop the arms to the ground. This con pletes the full cycle. The arm lift expands the chest by pulling o the chest muscles, arching the back, and relieving the weight on th chest.

The cycle should be repeated 12 times per minute at a stead uniform rate. The compression and expansion phases should occup about equal time; the release periods being of minimum duration. 

Additional Related Directions: 

It is all important that artificial respiration, when needed, be starte quickly. There should be a slight inclination of the body in such way that fluid drains better from the respiratory passage. The hea of the subject should be extended, not flexed forward, and the chi should not sag lest obstruction of the respiratory passages occur. check should be made to ascertain that the tongue or foreign object are not obstructing the passages. These aspects can be cared fo when placing the subject into position or shortly thereafter, betwee cycles. A smooth rhythm in performing artificial respiration is de sirable, but split-second timing is not essential. Shock should receiv adequate attention, and the subject should remain recumbent afte resuscitation until seen by a physician or until recovery seems assured (6) Continue artificial respiration without interruption until nature breathing is restored, if necessary, four hours or longer, or until physician declares the patient is dead. (7) As soon as this artificial respiration has been started and whil it is being continued, an assistant should loosen any tight clothin about the patient's neck, chest or waist. Keep the patient warm 

Do not give any liquids whatever by mouth until the patient is fully conscious. (8) To avoid strain on the heart when the patient revives, he should be kept lying down and not allowed to stand or sit up. If the docto has not arrived by the time the patient is revived, he should be given some stimulant, such as one teaspoonful of aromatic spirits of ammonia in a small glass of water, or a hot drink of coffee or tea, etc. The patient should be kept warm. (9) Resuscitation should be carried on at the nearest possible point to where the patient received his injuries. He should not be moved from this point until he is breathing normally, of his own volition and then moved only in a lying position. Should it be necessary, due to extreme weather conditions, etc., to move the patient before he is breathing normally, resuscitation should be carried on during the time that he is being moved. (10) A brief return of natural respiration is not a certain indication for stopping the resuscitation. Not infrequently the patient after a temporary recovery of respiration, stops breathing again. The patient must be watched, and if natural breathing stops, artificial respiration should be resumed at once.

GENERAL 

(11) The above described method covering the new Standard Tech ique for executing the Back Pressure-Arm Lift Method of Artificial lespiration has been approved by the American Red Cross and superdes the Schafer Prone-Pressure Method formerly exhibited in the ime Tables; however, if you have not been instructed in the new ack Pressure-Arm Lift Method and the emergency arises necessisting the need for use of Artificial Respiration, then use any known ethod. eneral Points to be Observed in all Cases Requiring Resuscitation. 

Take care of the patient. An unconscious person becomes cold very pidly, and chilling means a further strain on a vitality already eakened. Experience has shown that the cold to which the victims f electric shock are often carelessly exposed, is probably the most aportant cause of pneumonia, and this disease is the most dangerous fter-effect of the accident. As far as possible keep the patient covered ad warm both during and after resuscitation. Use hot pads, hot ater bottles, hot bricks, radiant heaters or other similar means, but member that an unconscious man has no way of telling you when he being burned.

Do not permit the patient to exert himself. If it should be necesry to move him, keep him lying down. fedicines and Medical Help. 

Never give an unconscious man anything to drink. It may choke m. Medical science knows no drug which of itself will start the reathing in a patient whose breathing has ceased. 

There is great danger of prematurely ceasing resuscitation. Breathg has been re-established after eight hours of resuscitation in cases electric shock. Therefore, the ordinary and general tests for death ould not be accepted, and any doctor should make several very areful examinations and be sure specific evidence, such as the onset rigor mortis, is present before the patient is pronounced dead and suscitation is stopped. 

he Action of the Electric Current. 

In electric shock the current may pass through the breathing center the base of the brain and cause this center to stop sending out the ervous impulses which act upon the muscles responsible for breathg. As a consequence, breathing stops abruptly. If the shock has ot been severe, after a time the breathing center recovers and reimes the vitally necessary duty of sending impulses to the muscles f breathing. In such cases the immediate use of the back pressurerm lift method substitutes this artificial breathing for the natural spiration of the patient. As has been pointed out, the current may paralyze the breathing center as to require eight hours for recovery nd the prone method must be used unceasingly through this entire me.

Victims of electric shock of this sort are unconscious, but in them he heart and blood circulation continue. Their treatment demands rtificial respiration with the greatest possible promptness. 

In some cases the electric current affects the heart. Under these ircumstances the heart suddenly ceases to pump blood. Many cases f electric shock escape this heart effect, and even an experienced xaminer requires time to assure himself it has occurred. Conseuently it is the duty of those first reaching the shocked person to ive artificial respiration by the back pressure-arm lift method at nce and to continue until natural breathing is restored or until the nset of rigor mortis. irst Care of Burns.

When natural respiration has been restored, burns, if serious, should e immediately attended to while waiting for the doctor to arrive. 

A raw or blistered surface should be protected from the air. If lothing sticks, do not peel it off-cut around it. The adherent loth, or a dressing of cotton, or other soft material applied to the burned surface, should be saturated with picric acid (0.5 per cent.). if this is not at hand, use a solution of baking soda (one teaspoonful To a pint of water), or the wound may be coated with a paste of flour Jnd water, or it may be protected with vaseline, carron oil, olive oil,

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

castor oil or machine oil, if clean. Cover the dressing with cott gauze, lint, clean waste, clean handkerchief or other soft cloth h lightly in place by a bandage.

The same covering should be lightly bandaged over a dry, char burn, but without wetting the burned region or applying oil on 9.

INSTRUCTIONS GOVERNING THE USE OF THE FIRE FIGHTING EQUIPMENT ON DIESEL LOCOMOTIVES. 

Fire Discovery: 

ENGINEMEN AND CREWMEN. 

1. Bring train to stop to reduce draft and shut off all engines. 

2. Pull emergency fuel cutout handle on unit affected. 

3. Snap off fuel pump switches. 

4. For electrical fire-Shut off current to unit affected.

Fire Extinguishers: 

1. CO2 (Carbon Dioxide) is the preferred extinguisher for use either oil or electrical fires. It can be identified by fan shaped ho or nozzle. Diesel locomotives are equipped with 10, 20 and 50 pour sizes. 

2. Pyrene (Carbon Tetrachloride)—Diesel Locomotives are equippe with 1 qt. size in cab and 1 gal. size in motor room.

Use of Extinguishers: 

CO2-Small sizes: Grip the handle of the horn, pull the safet pin and set off extinguisher by opening the valve on top of the cylinde or by squeezing handle together. Use close to fire as extinguisher h a maximum range of 8 feet.

CO2-Large Sizes: This extinguisher is portable by two men. Pu extinguisher from clamps and carry to any ocation needed. Ex tinguisher must be in upright position when used.

To Operate: Uncoil hose. Pull out lock pin. Open valve at to of cylinder. Control discharge from horn by valve provided on th horn handle. Discharge valve should be triggered to prevent th possibility of freezing the valve shut. When two men are availabl the other man should handle the cylinder valve. This extinguishe is most effective when operated close to the fire. After extinguishin fire close valve on cylinder and again open horn valve to release ga from hose.

The best results are obtained from CO2 extinguishers by playing the discharge as close to fire as possible, directing it at the base o the fire and moving it very slowly from side to side, gradually pro gressing forward or upward. Continue discharge until hot surface and glowing material is cool. 

Pyrene-1 qt. size: Pull extinguisher from bracket, turn the handle one-quarter turn counter-clockwise, and operate as a pump. Direct the stream of liquid at base of the fire.

Pyrene-1 gal. size: Bring extinguisher as close as possible to the fire. Hold the hose in one hand and open small valve at the back of the extinguisher. Direct stream of liquid at base of the fire. 

Fire in traction motors can be put out by either CO2 or Pyrene extinguisher. When CO2 extinguisher is used place extinguisher horn against opening in traction motor blower and expel CO2 gas into it. 

When Pyrene extinguisher is used discharge liquid into motor through exhaust ventilating duct located in the housing.

After Fire is Extinguished: 

1. Replace extinguisher. 

2. Furnish proper report, showing cause, action taken and extinguisher used. 

3. Restore to proper operating positions, emergency fuel cut-off. valve, fuel pump and electrical switches. of

INSTRUCTIONS FOR FIRE FIGHTING—(Con.). 

Maintenance of Extinguishers: 

1. Extinguisher must be kept accessible and clean. 

2. Foreign material must not be hung or stored on extinguishers. 

3. Know safety pin is in valve on CO² extinguishers and valve is operly sealed. 

4. Check pressure gauge on large Pyrene extinguishers; it should ow 100 lbs., examine liquid level through sight glass in front of tinguisher. 

5. Check small Pyrene extinguisher by lifting to determine fullness. 

6. Report any exceptions to condition of extinguisher. 

Warning Guides: 

1. Gases of combustions are toxic; therefore in confined places ter fire is extinguished, ventilate area. 

2. Contents of fire extinguishers on Diesel locomotives are noninductors of electricity. 

3. Never point extinguisher at fellow employe unless he is on fire; void unnecessary handling of carbon dioxide snow. 

4. Time is important after discovery of fire, so know in advance cations and use of extinguishers. 

5. A Diesel locomotive must not be stopped over any open flame, ght or fire, except in case of emergency, when immediate action ust be taken to prevent damage to the locomotive by fire. 

It is of the utmost importance that you be fully familiar with the se of the respective fire extinguishers and procedure to follow in case f fire on Diesel-electric locomotives. If you are in doubt as to proedure, get in touch with your Road Foreman or Asst. Road Foreman f Engines, Inspector of Safety, Diesel Supervisor, Master Mechanic r M. P. & R. E. Fire Chief.

RAILROAD RADIO. 

"All personnel whose duties require their operating a Base Station radiotelephone must take and pass an examination on the following Railroad Radio General and Operating Rules and Special Instruction. Personnel whose duties require their operating a mobile station only must understand and be conversant with these Rules and Special Instructions."

The instructions herein set forth govern "Railroad Radio" and over the use of radio systems on the railroads operated by the Reading Company and must be observed by all employes whose luties are in any way affected thereby. 

.General Rules. 

A. Definition: A Railroad Radio Communication System is one employing radio for the transmission of intelligence between moving equipment, between moving equipment and a fixed point, or between ixed points.

B. Radio communication systems are under the jurisdiction of the 

Federal Communications Commission. The Railroad Company and its employes are governed by the Commission's Operating Rules. 

Violation is a federal offense for which severe penalties are provided. 

C. In order to operate a radio transmitting set a railroad employe must read and study the following rules and pass an examination thereon. Such examinations will be given by railroad examiners. 

II. Operating Rules. 

1. All employes, except those specifically authorized to do so, are prohibited from making any adjustments_to_a_railroad radio set. 

Employes so authorized must carry their FCC Operator License or verification card when on duty. If it appears that a radio transmitter is not operating properly its use shall be discontinued and the designated railroad official notified as soon as possible. 

2. No employe shall knowingly transmit any false distress communication, any unnecessary, irrelevant or unidentified communication, nor utter any obscene, indecent, or profane language via radio. 

3. No employe shall divulge or publish the existence, contents, purport, effect or meaning of any communication (distress communication excluded) except to the person for whom the communication is intended or to another employe of the railroad whose duties may

RAILROAD RADIO.-(Con.). 

require knowledge of the communication.

The above applies either Hot to communications received direct or to any that may be intercepted 4. Before transmitting any employe operating a radio transmitting set shall listen a sufficient interval to be sure that the circuit is no already in use, particularly for distress traffic. 

5. A distress call will be preceded by the word "Emergency" re peated three times. Such calls shall be used only to cover initial reports of derailments, storms, washouts, fires, obstructions to tracks or other matters which would cause serious delay to traffic, damage to property, injury to employes or the traveling public, and shall contain as complete information thereon as possible. All employes shall give absolute priority to communications from another station in distress, and except in answering or aiding a station in distress shall refrain from sending any communications until there is assurance that no interference will result to the station in distress. 

6. The Railroad Company is required to answer an official notice of violation of the terms of the Communications Act of 1934, as amended, within three days from receipt of notice and any employe receiving inquiry concerning any violation shall answer such inquiry within 24 hours after receipt of notice. 

7. Any employe shall permit inspection of the radio equipment in his charge and all FCC documents pertaining thereto, by a duly. accredited representative of the Federal Communications Commission at any reasonable time. 

8. Employes shall identify the radio station from which they are calling by prefacing their call with the railroad name. (See General 

Instruction 17.) 

9. In certain cases at crossing, junctions or paralleling tracks some interference may develop with another railroad. In such cases especial care in making identification shall be used and the employes concerned shall cooperate in handling their business by alternating calls and. beingas brief as possible. 

10. If any communication from a station other than another railroad radio station interferes with Railroad Radio Service, the Railroad employe will endeavor to ascertain the identity of such station and report the occurrence as soon as possible through authorized channels to the designated railroad official, giving the exact time, nature of the communication and identity of the station, if possible. 

11. Internationally, the word "MAYDAY" indicates a distress message, the word "PAN" an urgent message, and the word "SECURITY", a safety message. Railroad employes may hear such messages sent by aircraft or, in coastal areas, by boats. Railroad employes hearing such messages must report them immediately through authorized channels to the designated railroad official in addition to taking such appropriate action to relieve the distress as may be possible.

III. General Instructions. 

1. Radiotelephone equipment consists of loud speakers, hand-set telephones, control units and associated apparatus, installed in cabs of diesel locomotives, other rolling equipment and at wayside stations. 

It also consists of portable units, carried manually, in vehicles, on trains, etc. Radiotelephone equipment is used for telephone communication between units so equipped. 

Radiotelephone is to be used only in connection with Company business and in the protection of life and property. It will be used between locomotive and caboose, train to train, between wayside radio offices and with portable sets whenever its use is in the interest of safe operation and will avoid delay to trains or damage to equipment. Its use should make it unnecessary for the conductor to apply air from the caboose except in cases of extreme emergency. Use for casual conversation and similar purposes is prohibited. 

2. Definitions: A BASE STATION is a permanently fixed loca tion at which the radio transmitting apparatus is located. 

MOBILE STATION. A station in a mobile service intended to be used while in motion or during halts at unspecified points. 

3. Base stations will be kept in service at all times unless otherwise provided. st

RAILROAD RADIO.-(Con.). 

4. Mobile stations, except diesel units and other equipment being 

Fauled dead in the train, must have the radiotelephone equipment in rvice between initial and final terminals unless otherwise provided. cases of emergency or at other times, radiotelephone apparatus cated on dead equipment may be used. 

5. Base stations will be shown in the timetable and the location of ontrol stations indicated. 

6. Local Control Units located in the cabs of locomotives and other quipment are installed for the purpose of controlling the mobile ransmitter and receiver and holding the telephone handset. ontain the following components: (A) A telephone handset suspended on a hanger. They (B) An amber colored light indicating transmitter "On the Air". (C) A yellow colored light indicating power is turned on to the equipment. (D) A volume control for controlling the level of sound to the handset receiver and the loudspeaker.

When the handset is suspended in its hanger the loudspeaker is in ervice. To use the transmitter, the handset is lifted off the hanger which automatically disconnects the loudspeaker. To transmit the

Push-to-talk" button on the handset is depressed and the amber ight is illuminated on the control unit. No reception can be had while this button is depressed. When the transmission is concluded he button is released and the amber light is extinguished, reception gain being possible through the handset receiver, only as long as the handset is off its hanger. To actuate the loudspeaker after the conversation is completed the handset is simply replaced on the hanger. 

To light the yellow light on the control unit and hence energize the radio equipment, the switch type circuit breaker located near the radio apparatus must be actuated by hand. 

7. Speech to the transmitter should be made with a clear, distinct tone of natural voice and slowly enough to be understood. 

8. A talk-test shall be made before leaving the specified terminal. 

Satisfactory two-way conversation shall serve as the test. Any failure shall be reported immediately to the engine house maintenance force on duty or to the designated railroad official. 

9. Terminals at which radio equipment is located for talk-tests are Bridgeport, Erie Avenue and Saucon Creek engine house. 

10. Any failure which occurs while on line of road shall be promptly reported to the designated railroad official. In addition locomotive radio failures shall be reported on the locomotive performance report for the trip. Conductors shall insure that failures of equipment other than that located in locomotives are reported to the engine house maintenance force upon reaching the terminal. 

11. Personnel at way stations must promptly report to the designated railroad official all failures of train radiotelephone equipment to function properly, as well as instances of inability to contact equipped mobile equipment. 

12. Employes using train radiotelephones in connection with train operations, must satisfy themselves that they are in communication with the proper persons and must not consider conversation finished until the persons taking part are assured that they have heard all of the conversation and that it is understood, repeating same when necessary. 

13. Persons using radiotelephones must yield the train radiotelephone channel promptly for communications concerning train movements. 

14. The train radiotelephone is to be used to report any irregular or unsafe conditions observed on trains or along right of way, and in any way that will benefit train operation, improve safety, or prevent accidents. 

15. It is important that all train radiotelephone calls be answered promptly, as train accidents may be avoided by such action. 

16. The train radiotelephone must be turned "OFF" at the end of trip or as otherwise provided by local instructions. 

17. Employes using radiotelephone_equipment shall, in addition to abiding by the Operating Rules for Railroad Radio, use the following prescribed radiotelephone procedure at all times:

SAY AGAIN

(C) In initiating a conversation the calling station states the called station first, followed by his own call and the word "over". (D) The following is an example of a complete conversation between a mobile and a wayside station: "Reading Woodbourne, this is Reading extra 507, over." "This is Reading Woodbourne, over." "This is Reading extra 507; I have 17 coal for Fairless. Advise disposition, over." "This is Reading Woodbourne; proceed to Fairless and take instructions from yardmaster there, over." "This is Reading extra 507; Roger, out." 

18. Brief information of emergencies should be given with the first call made; for example, "Emergency, emergency, emergency; Reading extra 257A this is Reading "JG" Tower-hotbox (plus further orders to stop train, etc., when deemed necessary), over". If prompt reply is not received, repeat the call giving details on the assumption that the receiver at "JG" Tower or the transmitter on the locomotive may have failed but that diesel 257A is receiving the information. 

This should be evidenced by normal application of brakes from head end. This procedure is applicable in other emergencies, to permit prompt action with minimum delay. 

19. Prompt answering, brief transmission and general courtesy on the air will greatly augment the usefulness of the radio telephone system and possibly save lives and property damage. 

20. In all the foregoing, the designated railroad official is the 

Division Superintendent unless otherwise provided.

Hellertown: "U" The limits of "BY" Interlocking have been extended in an Easterly direction to include interlocked crossover and protecting signals at Hellertown.

Facing point crossover with point of switch in Westward main track one hundred twenty (120) feet West of Hellertown Station will be controlled from "BY" Interlocking Station. Trains shall not exceed a speed of twenty (20) miles per hour while making movements over this crossover.

Power mechanisms on crossover switches are equipped with selector and switch operating lever for emergency operation, subject only to direction of Signalman at "BY" Interlocking Station.

When Signalman at "BY" Interlocking Switch issues instructions to train crew to operate switches by hand, selector lever must be reversed and switch can then be operated by switch operating lever. 

Upon completion of emergency operation, switches must be operated to normal position by switch operating lever and selector lever set normal and secured with padlock. 

Electric locks under the control of the Signalman at "BY" Interlocking Station will be placed in service at the following locations:

SPRING SWITCHES. 

WEST END OF DOUBLE TRACK. PT. READING. 

1. The normal position of switch is for eastward movement. 

2. Reflector sign "End of Double Track" is located along westard track three hundred twelve (312) feet east of switch and desigates clearance point between main tracks. 

3. Eastward color light distant switch signal is located ninety-six 

96) feet west of switch. Signal normally displays proceed indication. 

4. When signal displays a "PROCEED" indication, switch is in ormal position. 

5. When signal displays a "STOP" indication, crews must be govrned as follows: (a) Determine that tracks are not occupied west of clearance point. (b) Examine switch and if found reversed, operate hand ground lever to restore switch to normal position. (c) If found in normal position, but not fully closed, examine switch for obstruction between switch point and stock rail and if after reversing and again closing switch with ground lever, switch will not fully close and signal will not clear, immediately notify 

Chief Train Dispatcher. 

6. Trains shall not exceed a speed of twenty (20) miles per hour on vestward track while passing over turnout. 

MANVILLE. 

1. The normal position of switch is for westward movements. 

2. Reflector sign "End of Double Track" is located along eastward track three hundred ten (310) feet west of switch and designates clearance point between main tracks. 

3. Westward color light distant switch signal is located three hundred twenty (320) feet east of switch. Signal normally dark, is approach lighted. 

4. When signal displays a "PROCEED" indication, switch is in normal position. 

5. When signal displays a "STOP" indication, crews will be gov- 

Ferned as follows: (a) Determine that tracks are not occupied east of clearance point. (b) Examine switch and if found reversed, operate hand ground lever to restore switch to normal position. (c) If found in normal position but not fully closed, examine switch for obstruction between switch point and stock rail and if after reversing and again closing switch with ground lever, switch will not fully close and signal will not clear, immediately notify 

Chief Train Dispatcher. 

6. Trains shall not exceed a speed of twenty (20) miles per hour on eastward track while passing over turnout.

CAB SIGNAL RULES 

114. A four-indication cab signal system with engine and multipl unit car cab signals co-ordinated with wayside signal indications is i service between Jenkintown and Bethlehem, on the Bethlehem Branch of the Philadelphia Division. 

(a) The purpose of this equipment is to provide a continuous cal signal indication, together with means for automatically sounding warning whistle when a more restrictive cab indication is given, a well as means for acknowledging and suppressing sounding of th warning whistle. (b) Engines not equipped with required cab signals and supple mentary apparatus shall not be dispatched for movement over thi territory, except as follows: 

1. On written authority of the Superintendent. 

2. The following service with a speed restriction of 20 miles pe hour:

Switching Service: 

Between Jenkintown and Ambler. 

Between Jenkintown and Oreland. 

Between Jenkintown and Glenside. 

Between Hatfield and North Wales.

Between Bethlehem Station and east end of 

Saucon Creek Yard...

Work Train Service: 

Between Oreland and Jenkintown.. 

6.5 mile 3.0 1.1 4.7 3.4 10.6 茗米 ​3.0 3.4 and when necessary between Oreland and Lansdale. 

In Transfer and Drag service between Bethlehem and east end of Saucon Creek Yard... (c) Multiple unit cars not equipped with required cab signals and supplementary apparatus shall not be dispatched for movement as a single car train, or on head end of train of more than one car over this territory, except on written authority of the Superintendent. (d) Each equipped engine (with exception of DF-1 locomotives) has two complete four-indication cab signal units and supplementary whistle and acknowledger. DF-1 diesel locomotives have one (1) fourindication cab signal unit, located in a position between the two front windshields, where it can be observed by both engineman and fireman. 

Location of equipment is as follows: 

1. Equipment box on G-1, G-2, G-3 and I-9 steam locomotives is located ahead of firebox, on right side beneath footboard. 

On T-1 steam locomotives the equipment box is located on topdeck of tender, against the coal sheet.

On DF-1 diesel locomotives, the equipment box is located on left side, inside nose on "A" end.

On RS-1 diesel locomotives, the equipment box is located on ballast, in short end of hood.

On RS-1b diesel locomotives, equipment box is located ahead of cab, on right side, mounted on running board. 

On RS-2b diesel locomotives, equipment box is located in front hood on right side, beneath engine radiators.

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

2. Main Switch. 

On G-1, G-2, G-3, I-9 and T-1 steam locomotives, main switch is located at end of equipment box. 

On DF-1 diesel locomotives, main switch is located in front of cab on left side.

On RS-1, RS-1b and RS-2b diesel locomotives, main switch or switches marked "Cab Signal” are located on switch panel in cab.

There is an additional switch on equipment box on RS-1b diesel locomotives, however, this switch is to be kept in ON position at all times. 

3. Acknowledging switch.

On all locomotives conveniently located in cab for operation by engineman. 

4. Magnet valve.

On all steam locomotives, on rear wall of cab on right side near ceiling.

On DF-1 diesel locomotives, in nose on “A” end, on left side in front of cab. 

On RS-1 and RS-2b diesel locomotives, in short hood end of locomotives on cab wall.

On RS-1b diesel locomotives, at right side control stand. 

5. Cut-out cock.

On all locomotives, in air supply line close to magnet valve. 

6. Reversing switch.

On steam locomotives equipped, reversing switch is located on the right side cab ceiling near the acknowledging switch. 

Switch handle is placed in "UP" position for forward operation and in "DOWN" position for backward operation. 

On RS-1 diesel locomotives, reversing switch is located in cab on rear wall. Switch handle is placed in "UP" position for forward operation and in "DOWN" position for backward operation.

On RS-1b diesel locomotives, reversing switch is located on right side of cab beneath window. Switch handle must be turned in direction of operation.

On RS-2b diesel locomotives, the reversing switch is an integral part of the acknowledging switch. When the direction of operation is changed on dual-control locomotives, the handle must be removed from the acknowledging switch on the control side being vacated, and applied to the acknowledging switch on the control side to be used. This automatically places the proper receiver in operation for the direction desired. (e) When engineman first takes charge of a steam engine he will see that- 

1. Air compressor is operating. 

2. Turbo-generator is operating and globe valve in full open position. 

3. Main switch is closed. This will be indicated by a red cab signal. 

4. Cut-out cock in warning whistle air supply pipe is in full open position. 

5. Operate acknowledging switch to test operation and suppression of blowing of warning whistle. When acknowledging switch handle is held in acknowledging position, whistle will continue to blow until handle is released.

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

(f) When engineman first takes charge of a diesel locomotive, will see that- 

1. There is air pressure on locomotive. 

2. Main switch is closed. This will be indicated by a red ca signal. 

3. Cut-out cock in warning whistle air supply is in full op position. 

4. Operate acknowledging switch to test operation and suppre sion of blowing of warning whistle. When acknowledgin switch handle is held in acknowledging position, whistle w continue to blow until handle is released. 

5. Throw reversing switch to position desired for direction operation. (g) Before leaving engine terminal, engine crew must make t required departure tests, and it must be shown that all cab sign units are operating simultaneously. Engine shall be placed on te track, proceeding as follows: 

1. While engine is standing on test track, fireman will dismou from the engine and press push button switch located relay box adjacent to the departure test track. 

2. When push button is pressed it energizes the test track a cab signals should show a green light. 

3. After the signal has been held for a few seconds, cab signa will change to a yellow over green light. After warni whistle starts blowing, move acknowledging switch to a knowledging position, and release. Warning whistle shou sound until acknowledgment has been made and handle h been released. 

4. After a few seconds, a yellow light will be obtained, and war ing whistle should sound until acknowledgment has be made and handle released. 

5. After a few seconds, a red light will be obtained, and warni whistle should sound until acknowledgment has been ma and handle released. (h) Equipped engines must not enter cab signal territory witho having cut-out cock fixed in cut-in position. (i) Operation over non-equipped territory. 

After completion of departure test, if engine is moved fro engine terminal over territory not equipped for cab sign operation, a red light will show on cab signals.

When engine is equipped with cab signal and supplementa apparatus is dispatched on a run, no part of which is ca signal territory, the cut-out cock in the warning whistle & supply pipe will be closed and main switch on equipme box opened. (3) Each equipped MU car has a four-indication cab signal wi supplementary whistle and acknowledger. Location of equipment covered in Paragraphs 61 to 66, inclusive, in MU Car Instructi Book. (k) Before leaving terminals, MU Train Crews must make t required departure tests, and it must be known that the cab signa and warning whistle are working properly.

Reading Terminal. 

Terminal tracks 1 to 12 inclusive are equipped with test loo housed in trunking run parallel to and between rails. 

Push buttons for control of test loops are provided in small ir cases attached to pipe standards located in platforms adjacent to a between tracks.

Test loops are wired in two groups, the one_comprising loops tracks 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5, and the other in tracks 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 1

A pilot lamp is provided in each control box, which when lighte indicates that test is being made on one of the tracks in the grou Test must not be started when pilot lamp in control box is lighte To start test:-Hold push button in depressed position until pil lamp indicates that test is under way. Cycle of test is as follows:

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

1. Green indication displayed in cab signal unit. 

2. After the signal has been held for a few seconds, cab signal will change to a yellow over green light. After warning whistle starts blowing, move acknowledging switch to acknowledging position and release. Warning whistle should sound until acknowledgment is made and switch is released. 

3. After a few seconds, a yellow light will be obtained, and warning whistle should sound until acknowledgment has been made and switch released. 

4. After a few seconds, a red light will be obtained, and warning whistle should sound until acknowledgment has been made and switch released.

Cars under test must be placed within limits of loop and with both ucks of car west of insulated track joints located approximately ghty (80) feet north of bumper blocks. In the event of failure to otain proper test, car should be moved a few feet and test repeated. ail surface must be clear of sand to permit proper test of cab signal paratus.

In the event of failure to receive proper test of cab signal apparatus ith or without pilot lamp lighted after push button has been deressed: 

Crew shall immediately repeat the test with one of the rear cars in train or on track; failure to receive proper test when more than one car on a track is tested indicates that the wayside test apparatus is defective.

Failure of test on first car of train followed by proper operation when test is made on another car on the same track indicates a defect in car apparatus on first car. 

Conductor of train must immediately report the failure to Chief 

Train Dispatcher from telephone located on inside of train shed fence adjacent to Gate No. 7. 

Chief Train Dispatcher, after being advised of circumstances, shall, when time permits, arrange to have car tested on another track if failure is indicated as being caused by defective testing apparatus, or in the case of a defective car to substitute another MU car.

Time or conditions not permitting arrangements as outlined in preceding paragraph, without excessive delay, train shall be dispatched on written authority of Superintendent, Chief

Train Dispatcher to be governed by instructions issued by 

General Superintendent, dated November 3, 1936, pertaining to operation of equipped locomotives on which the cab signals have become inoperative.

The same general requirements for Departure tests obtain at 

Hatfield, Doylestown, Lansdale and Bethlehem, except that operating instructions for the types of test apparatus installed at outlying points are posted in cases housing apparatus. (1) Cab signals will not indicate track conditions when: 

1. Engine is moving backward, except engines equipped for both forward and reverse operation. - 2. Outside of Cab Signal territory. 

3. Moving against current of traffic. 

4. Engine is pushing cars. (m) Both engineman and fireman shall observe and by communica- Sion with each other establish that the cab signal system has cut in nd both sets of signals are operating properly. (n) If cab signals or supplementary apparatus fails to cut in when ntering cab signal territory, same must be considered a failure. 

115. CAB SIGNALS. 

(a) Engineman must not operate the acknowledging lever until after signal indication has been observed and is being obeyed. (b) In cab signal territory, if the cab signal whistle sounds more than six seconds, fireman on engine will immediately go to the engineman.

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

(c) When cab signal or supplementary apparatus fails to cut in becomes inoperative in cab signal territory and repairs cannot I made by the engineman, he may, if necessary, cut out indicat whistle. 

On locomotives, engineman shall inform fireman when cut-out made.

Fireman shall arrange to note indication of all wayside signals, ca. ing same to engineman when practicable.

On MU cars engineman shall immediately notify conductor wh shall then place a trainman on the operating end of the car wit engineman. Trainman shall note indication of all wayside signal calling same to the engineman.

The train may then proceed at one-half the authorized speed bu not exceeding thirty miles per hour to the first point of communic tion and failure reported to and instructions received from th superintendent. (d) Any defect in, or failure of cab signals or supplementary appa ratus must be reported by message at the first open telegraph offic at which train stops. 

116. The Laws of the State of New Jersey provide in part a 

A bell of a weight not less than thirty pounds shall be placed o each engine and rung continuously in approaching a grade crossing a highway, beginning at a distance of at least three hundred yard from the crossing and continuing until the engine has crossed suc highway, or a steam whistle shall be attached to engine and b sounded, except in cities, at least three hundred yards from the cros ing and at intervals until the engine shall have crossed the highway Also:-It shall be unlawful for any employe of any steam or ele tric railroad company to operate any locomotive or train, or crossin gate in such a manner as to unnecessarily prevent or interfere wit the use of any highway for the purpose of travel.

Passengers for Reading Compan points to continue through to Wayne Junction and use first connectin service from that point.

NOTE A.-If L. V. No. 4 is too late for No. 356, 358, 312, 2312, 1312 r 2306 and would require wait of more than 30 minutes for No. 316, leepers should be operated extra Bethlehem to Philadelphia. Extra to top to let off passengers at any intermediate point to Lansdale also at 

Lansdale, Jenkintown, Wayne Junction or North Broad Street if ecessary.

If and when L. V. No. 8 is running late, and cannot be delivered within the holding time of ten minutes, then the time can be extended twenty minutes additional, or a maximum of thirty minutes, providing there is a definite and complete understanding between the Chief Dispatcher of the Phila. Div. and the Lehigh Valley R. R. After the expiration of 30 minutes, the L. V. R. R. connection is to be run special, up to and within 30 minutes time of the departure of No. 316; after which it is to be operated in No. 316.

Conductors of C. N. J. train should notify Agent at Bethlehem, who in turn should notify yardmaster, Reading Co. Bethlehem Station so that 

Reading Co. train may be held.

Wayne Junction, matter of holding Reading Co. trains at Bethlehem should be given special consideration.

127. When necessary to make movements against current of traf within yard limits, permission in writing must be secured, using For TD-116. Such instructions shall designate by name the points b tween which movements are to be made and in executing same, trai and engines will move with caution.

Form TD-116 must be written in manifold, delivering copy to eac employe addressed. Copy must be retained by each yardmaste towerman, and employe involved. Before issuing Form TD-110 yardmaster and/or towerman must know that the track upon whic movement is to be made has been cleared of opposing trains an movement is fully protected.

"Yard Speed"—A speed that will permit stopping within one-half the range of vision,

Passenger trains carrying freight cars not equipped for passenger service must not exceed speed for symbol trains, unless otherwise instructed. Work trains without crane may operate at speed authorized for symbol trains, unless otherwise instructed. 

Work trains with crane or with pivoted machine must not exceed speed for relief trains. 

Movements to and from tracks equipped with electric locked switches and/or derails located within interlocking limits that are not signalled, must be made at restricted speed. ..

142. (a) Subway crews must keep red lamp lighted on their engine day and night. (b) No switching must be done in Subway Tunnel while passenger trains are passing through. (c) Flagman of trains running via Pennsylvania Avenue Subway must observe Rule 1463.

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

(d) Employes using hand lamps or torches in Subway Tunnel or either entrance must conceal them from view of Enginemen of trai passing through or approaching the Tunnel. They must also ca fully avoid obstructing Engineman's view of the Tunnel signals. (e) Before a movement can be made from the Willow and Not Street Branch to the Subway Branch at 13th Street telephone perm sion must be secured from the Yardmaster at 17th Street. If unab to secure telephone permission such movement may only be ma under flag protection. (f) Engines or trains must not cross from one main track to t other in Penna. Avenue Tunnel without first securing permission fro 

Yardmaster at 17th Street or Park Jct.

(h) MU, Gas-Electric and Oil-Electric Switcher engines may operat on tracks Nos. 6, 11, 12 and 13 in Reading Terminal Train Shed. A other classes of engines are prohibited from operating on these track

Branch, is restricted to all cars having a gross weight in excess

143. (a) On the American Street Line, on the Willow Street Branch and on the Belt Line, except where there is a Crossing Watchma on duty, engine and car movements over grade crossings must b protected by a member of the crew in advance. (6) Engine and train movements over Front and Brown Stree

Crossing must not exceed two (2) miles per hour and must be pre ceded by member of train crew with proper flagging equipment. (c) Crossing Watchman will be on duty at Front and Willow Streets 

American Street Line, Philadelphia, Pa., from 9:00 A. M. to 5:00 

P. M., 2nd and Germantown Avenue and 2nd and Girard Avenue 

2:00 P. M. to 10:00 P. M., Monday to Friday, inclusive, except holidays.

When Crossing Watchman is not on duty, engine and train move ments over this crossing must not exceed two (2) miles per hour and must be preceded by member of train crew with proper flagging equipment. 

144. When necessary to place cars on No. 1 Sand House at 20th St.

Subway (track parallel to eastward main), be sure that cars are at least four (4) car lengths into clear of outlet switch. 

145. No engines or cars are to stand on curve of River Track at

Fountain Green, between inlet switch and overhead signal bridge located about four thousand (4000) feet west of Belmont Tower.

When necessary to cut trains on westward main track to set cars off on River Track at Fountain Green, be sure that cut is made so that no cars will be west of overhead bridge. 

146. Cab storm windows (windshields) on I-9, I-10, K and T-1

Engines must be kept in closed position when operating on leg of Wye between "FS" and "WV" Interlocking Stations, West Falls, and through Manayunk Tunnel. 

147. Trains must not cross over between West Manayunk and

Woodlane without first obtaining permission from Train Dispatcher. 

148. Automatic signals Nos. 179 and 190 at West and East ends of

Manayunk Tunnel are positive and must not be passed while stop is displayed without securing release from Train Dispatcher by telephone. Opposing freight trains and trains consisting of passenger equipment not carrying passengers are permitted to pass through tunnel at same time but regular and extra passenger trains and relief trains must not under any conditions proceed through Manayunk 

Tunnel without securing permission from the Train Dispatcher by telephone.

R. located four hundred twenty (420) feet east of Luzerne Street placed in service.

A member of the train crew, equipped with a red flag during dayght hours and with a red lantern and a white lantern during the hours darkness, must precede each and every movement of each locootive, car or train across Second Street on this side track and properly arn the traveling public of the approach of such locomotive, car train.

When necessary to sound the horn of Diesel Locomotives within ty limits the low tone will be used except in an emergency. 

150. (a) When signals Y31 and Y42 are in stop position, trains hall not proceed except by orders from Yardmaster at Erie Avenue. (b) Telephone to Yardmaster's office is located at base of mast of astward signal No. Y31. 

151. (a) Between Somerset Street and Venango Street, on the Port ichmond Branch, main tracks may be used with the current of affic, without protecting against following trains or yard engines. 

On all tracks between these points, trains and yard engines must tot exceed yard speed. 

Unless otherwise instructed by Yardmaster at Fairhill Junction, astward trains will stop at Venango Street and communicate with ardmaster at Fairhill Junction by telephone located in Crossing Watchman's cabin at that point for instructions before proceeding. 

Permission must be obtained from Yardmaster at Fairhill Junction efore crossing from one main track to the other in this territory. (b) An overhead crane is constructed over and above the Kettlewell treet Branch which serves the Pennsylvania Smelting and Refining ompany. Standard clearance is provided except when crane is in peration.

While crane is in operation a red banner by day, and a red light y night, will be posted at points one hundred fifty (150) feet east nd west of the overhead crane. When the warning signs are posted, rains must not pass under the overhead crane until the conductor as proper understanding with employe operating crane and crane 

8 in to clear.

All crews operating on the Kettlewell Street Branch shall be on the lert, expecting to find the overhead crane in operation.

NEW YORK BRANCH. 

153. Crossing gates which when in lowered position block the right hand highway approaches, and flasher signals, both automatically operate in service where three (3) main tracks of Reading Company cross at grade public highway, known as Hollow Road, located twenty-nine hundred (2900) feet west of Skillman Station, New 

York Branch.

Gates and signals will be actuated automatically for current of traffic movements on each of the four main tracks. Movement of trains shall be subject to requirements of General Rule T, Rules for the Government of the Operating Department. 

In order to avoid excessive warning periods and blocking of highway traffic, when trains on track 4 stop west of crossing, cut engine and proceed to Skillman for water, a push button cut-out and sign with legend "Eastward trains cut here" has been provided, adjacent to track 4 and located four hundred (400) feet west of the Hollow Road Crossing.

In order to avoid blocking grade crossings at Possumtown Road located one thousand nine hundred and seven (1907) feet east of 

Stoutsburg, and at Hollow Road located two thousand nine hundred (2900) feet west of Skillman, eastward trains stopping to take water

SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS-(Con.). 

at Skillman will arrange to stop with rear end of train clear of Possu town Road, and if head end is east of point four hundred (400) fe west of Hollow Road crossing, sufficient cars must be cut off at si adjacent to track 4 with legend "Eastward trains cut here." 

With engine and train stopped on track 4, west of sign, push butt cut-out must be operated, gates will rise and flashers will stop aft engine moves east and clears crossing, provided there are no trai approaching on any other track. 

Crossing must be flagged for movement of engine returning to he end of train.

When eastward movement on track 4 approaches crossing aft cut-out has been operated, the gates and signals will operate aut matically to warn highway traffic when head end of train pass location of sign and cut-out button four hundred (400) feet west crossing. CHESTER BRANCH. 

180. Trains running east of Darby Creek must keep a sharp lookout for yard engines working between there and Marcus Hook. 

181. Approach drawbridges at Darby and Chester Creeks under control, prepared to stop if clear signal is not displayed. 

182. (a) Crossing gate and a mechanically locked derail will be installed at Price Street highway crossing at Stauffer Chemical Co., Chester, Pa.

Crossing gate must be lowered and across the highway to unlock derail before the derail can be operated to permit train movement 

Over the highway.

After movement over crossing is completed, derail must be restored to derailing position before gate is raised. 

Each movement of a locomotive, car or train over the crossing shall be protected by a member of crew, equipped with a red flag during daylight hours, and a red light and a white light during the hours of darkness, to properly warn the traveling public.

194. In order to protect the traction motors and avoid damage to electrical equipment, it will be necessary when operating Diesel- 

Electric Locomotives, Rail Motor Cars or Multiple Unit cars through water at or above the top of rail head, to strictly adhere to the following instructions, noting particularly that under no circumstances should Multiple Unit cars pass through water when above top of rail. 

DIESEL LOCOMOTIVES. 

195. Under no circumstances shall a Passenger, Freight or Switching Diesel Locomotive be operated through water in excess of three (3) inches above the rail head. When passing through water, do not exceed a speed of 3 M.P.H.

RAIL MOTOR CARS. 

196. Under no circumstances shall a Rail Motor Car be operated through water in excess of one (1) inch above the rail head. When passing through water, do not exceed a speed of 2 M.P.H.

MULTIPLE UNIT CARS. 

197. Absolutely under no circumstances should Multiple Unit Cars pass through water when above top of rail. If water approaches top of rail, Train Dispatcher and Power Dispatcher should be notified. 

During high winds "MU" trains drop pantographs passing over 

Delaware River Bridge.

Pantographs must not be raised or lowered against contact wire when standing beneath overhead bridges unless it would be impossible to move train otherwise.
